# Bad units: declared success, not seated

Generated by `python -m experiments.v02f_evidence` from committed results/benchmark_v0.2d (seed 20261123), benchmark_v0.2d_rep (20261124) and benchmark_v0.2e (20261130). v0.2d and its replication ran the v0.2d code (42df5c8); v0.2e ran the v0.2e code (ddd0d34). Simulation only.

A bad unit is a row with `declared_success` True and `gt_seated` False. On good-part trials it is a connector the cell would ship as good that the ground truth scores out of tolerance. On deliberately bad parts it is a bad part passed. For controllers that declare (C, D, F, G, H, I, J) this set equals the `escape` count of the benchmark tables. A, B and E never declare and are not listed.

Failed checks come from the four logged pose columns (`failed_checks` in the script): `depth` (depth < socket depth - 0.5 mm), `yaw` (|yaw| > 3 deg), `tilt` (tilt > 3 deg), `lateral` (radial offset > sqrt 2 x the per-axis limit, so certain), `lateral?` (radial offset between the limit and sqrt 2 x the limit: the code checks per axis, the radius alone cannot tell), and `not logged` (the pose passes every check it shows, so the failure is the settled check, the latched check, or a per-axis lateral fail; none is logged). As-run tolerances: depth 0.5 mm, lateral clearance + 0.15 mm per axis, tilt and yaw 3 deg (README 'Success tolerances': an assumed seating specification).
